## §316 v1.133.3 (R97-030) — Mobile apps: "Sync error"

Cause: `app/col.php` builds its constant `COL_SELECT` from the ledger amount constants of `app/pi.php` (R95-004, v1.119.0). The web front controller was changed then to load `pi.php` first; the headless loader of the app service (`api/v1/bootstrap.php`) still loaded `col` before `pi`, so every request to `/customer/api/v1/…` ended in a fatal error (HTTP 500, empty body) and the apps reported "Sync error". The web portal was not affected.

Fix: `api/v1/bootstrap.php` loads `pi` before `col`, and `col.php` itself now does `require_once pi.php`, so no entry point depends on the order again. No database step, no app update.

Tested: sign-in and every read route plus `/sync` for the Service Engineer, Golden PM and Golden HR apps with an administrator, a project manager and an HR account — all HTTP 200 (and the expected 403 where the account may not use an app).

**Security review**: no change of access rules; the failure exposed nothing (errors are not displayed, the body was empty). Added: the deploy now calls the app service once after applying a release (`/api/v1/me` without a token must answer 401) and fails when it does not — one request per deploy, nothing at run time.

Language keys 9 349 each.

## §317 v1.134.0 (R97-031) — System Budgets: red mark when the profit is below the minimum

`app/sb.php`: `sb_min_profit()` (setting `sb_min_profit`, 40 when not set, 0 = off), `sb_profit_pct()`, `sb_low()` (profit on cost below the minimum, 0,05 points tolerance for rounding), `sb_low_mark()` (the red ▼ with the figures in its tooltip). The basis is the markup on cost — the percentage typed on the part lines — so a budget at the standard 40 % is exactly on the line and not marked.

Where it shows: `views/system_budget.php` — notice at the top of the Totals card (red when the total is below, grey when only lines are), red Markup on cost for Labour, Parts and Total, ▼ next to the sale of each labour and part line below the minimum (expenses and excluded parts are not marked); `views/system_budgets.php` — ▼ next to the sale in the list, the legend, and for administrators / management the field "Minimum profit on cost %" (action `sb_min_profit`, gate `sb_can_reopen()`); `views/system_budget_convert.php` — the fourth figure is now Markup on cost (margin on sale below it), red with a notice when the offer would be below the minimum. Warning only: finishing and converting are not blocked. No database step.

**Security review**: the new action is behind the staff check, the CSRF token and the management gate (a project manager's post is refused — tested); the value is parsed as a number and clamped to 0–900; output escaped. The cost figures appear only on pages that already show costs to the same people. No finding.

Language keys 9 360 each.

## §318 v1.134.1 (R97-032) — Sub-project drop-down follows the chosen project

Cause: the task form narrows its sub-project list by hiding the `<optgroup>` of the other projects, but the searchable drop-down of `assets/portal.js` (R30-007) only skipped options that are hidden themselves — so its list still showed every sub-project. The Planning form did not narrow at all.

Fix: `portal.js` treats an option of a hidden group as hidden and prints the group label as a heading in the list (`.ss-group`); `views/task_edit.php` also hides and disables the options themselves; `views/planning.php` gets `subNarrow()` (project chosen → only its sub-projects; a sub-project of another project is cleared). No database step, no new language keys.

Tested with three projects in a browser: for each project chosen, sub-projects of other projects offered = 0 on task, ticket, function check, service report, inspection report, offer, production order, direct invoice, purchase order, requisition, planning, accounting reports and external service report — in the native list and in the search list.

**Security review**: display only; the server still checks on save that the sub-project belongs to the project. No finding.

Language keys 9 360 each.

## §319 v1.135.0 (R97-033) — Organisations: separate ERP codes for customers and suppliers

The ERP (SoftOne) keeps customers and suppliers in two ledgers with their own code series. The portal had three stores — `companies.erp_code`, `customers.erp_code`, `supplier_details.erp_code` — and copied one into the other: the organisation form wrote one code to all three (`co_sync_roles`), the supplier card and the supplier import wrote the supplier code to `companies.erp_code`, and linking a customer to a company copied in both directions (`org_link`).

Now: `companies.erp_code` = `customers.erp_code` = the **customer** code; `supplier_details.erp_code` = the **supplier** code. `views/companies.php` has the two fields (`erp_code`, `sup_erp_code`); `co_save_from_post()` saves the supplier code only with the role Supplier and refuses a supplier code that another organisation has (`co.dup_sup_erp`), as does `sup_save_from_post()`; `co_sync_roles()`, `sup_save_from_post()`, `sup_import_apply` and `org_link()` no longer copy across; `org_matches()` / `org_dupes()` read the customer code only, so a supplier number never matches a customer. `org_erp_review()` / `org_erp_move()` (action `org_erp_move`, gate `org_can()`): the card "ERP codes to tidy" on Contacts › Organisations — supplier-only organisations with a code in the customer field (moved in one go) and customer-and-supplier organisations with the same code on both sides (by hand). No database step.

**Security review**: new action behind the staff check, CSRF and `org_can()`, listed in `PA_ACTIONS`; all statements bound; output escaped. No finding.

Language keys 9 375 each.

## §320 v1.135.1 (R97-034) — Accounting reports: correcting the invoice number or date

`acc_invoice_refs()` lists the invoices registered on a report; `acc_correct_invoice()` (action `acc_correct`, accounting department only — `acc_is_accounting()`) changes `invoice_ref` / `invoiced_at` of the lines invoiced under the old number, re-links `invoice_id` to the ledger invoice with the new number when one exists, rewrites the report's `invoice_ref` list and `invoice_date`, updates the `invoice_ref` of the invoiced timesheets, and writes the event `corrected` with old → new — in one transaction. States, hours and values are not touched. `views/acc_report.php`: folded card "Correct the invoice number or date" with one row per invoice. No database step.

Tested: change of number and date; no change refused; invalid date refused; unknown number refused; a project manager does not see the card and his post is refused.

**Security review**: accounting gate on the server, CSRF, bound statements, the date validated with `checkdate`, every correction in the report history with the user. No finding.

Language keys 9 385 each.

## §321 v1.136.0 (R97-035) — Accounting: to invoice per vessel

New page `?p=acc_vessel[&vessel=N]` (`views/acc_vessel.php`, gate `acc_visible()`, vessels limited by `tk_scope()`); the vessel names of "Waiting to be reported" on Accounting and a new button lead to it. `acc_vessel_due()` in `app/acc.php` gathers, for the projects of the vessel: open register lines per project / sub-project; accounting reports in draft, returned, issued, sent or partly invoiced; sub-projects and project-level offers with an approved offer not fully invoiced (`inv_compute_many()` — contract, invoiced, remaining; "final" when the sub-project or project is completed; a manual "fully invoiced" override removes the row); proformas issued / paid whose gross is not covered by linked invoices (`PI_INV_SQL`), with the advances received; and the parts typed "I" on service reports that are not invoiced (`fsr_works.parts`). Read-only: no new action, no database step. Amounts appear only with `fin_visible()` or `acc_is_accounting()`.

**Security review**: read-only page behind the staff check and the accounting gate; the vessel id is checked against the vessels the user may see; one bound parameter and integer-cast id lists in the statements; output escaped; amounts masked for people without the finance view. No finding.

Language keys 9 427 each.

## §322 v1.137.0 (R97-036) — Offers: estimated delivery time for parts and labour

Migration `install/migrate_cp_r97_036_offer_delivery_time.sql`: `offers.delivery_parts`, `offers.delivery_labour` (VARCHAR 160, free text). Guard `ob_delivery_has()`. `views/offer_edit.php` shows the two fields for builder offers; `of_save_from_post()` stores them (new and edited offers); `pi` conversion budgetary → firm copies them. `ob_vars()` offers `{delivery_parts}` / `{delivery_labour}` to the text blocks. `ob_delivery_section()` builds the section "Delivery time" (title + one line per filled field); `ob_render_pdf()` and `ob_render_docx()` print it before the Validity block. When a text block of the offer already contains one of the two placeholders, the automatic section is not printed (`delivery_in_text`), so nothing appears twice.

Tested: saved through the form; PDF and Word show "6. Delivery time" with both lines between Exclusions and Validity; numbering of the following sections follows.

**Security review**: two text fields, trimmed to 160 characters, bound parameters, written only by the people who may edit the offer; printed as text in PDF / Word (no HTML). No finding.

## §323 v1.138.0 (R97-037) — Parts of delivery notes go to accounting

Migration `install/migrate_cp_r97_037_dn_invoicing.sql`: `inv_dn_lines.inv_state` (`open` | `invoice` | `offer` | `invoiced`, default `open`), `inv_price` (unit sale price), `inv_ref`, `inv_date`, `inv_by`, `inv_name`, `inv_at`, `dec_by`, `dec_name`, `dec_at`, index on `inv_state`. Guard `dni_has()`.

`app/dn.php`: `dni_rows()` / `dni_counts()` read the lines of issued delivery notes of kind `project` or `customer` (vessel = the note's vessel, else the project's; cost = unit price of the stock movement, else the item's last price); `dni_decide_from_post()` (action `dni_decide`, gate `pr_admin()`) sets to-be-invoiced / included-in-the-offer / undecided with the unit price and notifies the accounting accounts; `dni_invoice_from_post()` (action `dni_invoice`, gate `acc_is_accounting()`) registers invoice number and date on the ticked waiting lines; `dni_back_from_post()` (action `dni_back`, accounting) returns waiting lines to procurement or invoiced lines to waiting. Page `?p=dn_invoicing[&state=][&vessel=]` (`views/dn_invoicing.php`) with the four tabs; buttons on Delivery notes and on Accounting; section "Parts on delivery notes" on the per-vessel page. The proposed price is cost + the minimum markup of the System Budgets (40 % unless changed).

Tested: decide (invoice with a typed price, offer, left undecided); a project manager without the procurement right gets 403 on the page and his post changes nothing; invoice registration; undo; production delivery notes never listed.

**Security review**: three new actions behind the staff check, CSRF and their role gates on the server; ids cast to integers, values bound; states from a fixed list; the unit cost is shown only to people with the procurement view. No finding.

## §324 v1.138.1 (R97-038) — Website statistics: networks and organisations

Decision of 14/11/2026: organisation level only — no IP address stored.

Website (repository `goldenas-website`, release R15): `api/_stats.php` gains `st_org_domain()` (reverse DNS of the visitor address, confirmed forwards so a self-set PTR name is not trusted, reduced to the registrable domain) and `st_org_record()`; `api/hit.php` calls it once per new visitor per day **after** the response was sent (`fastcgi_finish_request` / `litespeed_finish_request`), so the look-up never delays a page. Stored as a daily aggregate in `st_day`: dim `org`, k = `CC/domain` (`(no name)` when the connection publishes none), n = u = visitors. The feed (`mode=rows`) carries it without change. `privacy.html` → R4 with the added sentence (Italian dictionary updated).

Portal: `views/web_stats.php` — card "Networks and organisations" (`ws_top('org')`, 30 rows, domain + country + visitors). `ws_pull()` needed no change (dimension names are validated by pattern). No database step.

Limits: a domain is shown only when the network publishes a reverse-DNS name that also resolves back; home and mobile connections show the provider; the company name behind a network without its own name is not known (that would need an external address-owner register, which is not used).

**Security review**: the address is used for one DNS look-up and discarded; the name is validated as a host name, forward-confirmed and cut to 70 characters before it is stored, and escaped when shown; resolver time-out reduced and the request limited to 6 s after the response; the existing per-address rate limit of the beacon applies. No finding.

Language keys 9 479 each.

## §325 v1.139.0 (R97-039) — Web Sales › Newsletters

Decisions of 14/11/2026: editor in the portal, sending through Microsoft 365 (the portal's Graph connection), individual statistics, unsubscribe link in every message, AI assistance, sign-up on the website.

Migration `install/migrate_cp_r97_039_newsletters.sql`: `nl_issues` (blocks and audience as JSON, status draft / sending / sent / cancelled), `nl_sends` (one row per recipient: token, status, opened_at / opens, clicked_at / clicks, unsub_at), `nl_links`, `nl_clicks`, `nl_subs` (website subscribers, pending / confirmed / unsubscribed), `nl_optout` (unsub / bounce / manual), `nl_images`. Check key `r97p`.

`app/nl.php`: rights `nl_can()` (administrators, management, sales) and `nl_can_send()` (administrators, management); `nl_audience()` — active contacts with an e-mail of organisations by role (primary organisation and `contact_companies`), confirmed website subscribers by topic, extra addresses; opt-outs removed, one row per address, ceiling 5 000; `nl_render()` — table-based HTML e-mail, 600 px, navy / gold, logo from `assets/`; text escaped, `**bold**`, http(s) addresses linked; `nl_queue()` registers the links and the recipients and locks the issue; `nl_send_batch()` — called by `nf_sweep()` and by "Send next batch": claims rows, sends one message per recipient with `cp_graph_call(POST /users/{mailbox}/sendMail, saveToSentItems false)`, respects `nl_batch` (15) and `nl_daily` (400), stops on a token or throttling error; `nl_stats()`; `nl_mark()` (opt-out, also clears the newsletter / events subscriptions of the contact); `nl_ai_draft()` — `ai_run('write', …, 'newsletter')` with the brief, the audience groups and optionally 25 Marine intelligence headlines, answer parsed as JSON into blocks.

Public endpoint `customer/nl.php` (no session): `?o=` open pixel, `?c=&l=` click → redirect to the stored address of that link only, `?u=` unsubscribe page and POST, `?s=` confirmation of a website subscription, `?i=` picture. Links to goldenas.com.gr get `utm_source=newsletter&utm_campaign=nl<id>`, so Website statistics shows the visits per issue. `web_feed.php?r=subscribe` (site key) → `nl_pub_subscribe()`: pending row and confirmation e-mail, same answer whatever the state of the address.

Views `newsletters.php` (issues, website subscribers, unsubscribed and bounced, sending settings) and `newsletter.php` (editor, assistant, audience, preview, test, send, statistics); tab in `web_sales_tabs()`.

Limits: Microsoft Graph does not accept a `List-Unsubscribe` header, so the unsubscribe is the link in the message; bounces are not read automatically (no mailbox read permission) — marked by hand; replies arrive in the sending mailbox; Microsoft 365 is not a bulk-mail service — keep the daily ceiling moderate.

**Security review**: tokens are 24 random letters / digits (not hex), compared by exact match; the click endpoint redirects only to an address stored for that issue (open-redirect attempt tested); unsubscribe changes state only by POST; public endpoint rate-limited, sets no cookie, answers nothing personal; pictures checked by content, stored under `storage/nl` and served by token; e-mail HTML built from escaped text only (script in a block is printed as text — tested); link addresses must be http(s) (`javascript:` refused — tested); sending rights checked on the server (a project manager gets 403 / no change — tested); the assistant never receives contact data; sign-up needs the site key and double opt-in. Proposal: a dedicated mailbox (e.g. news@) for newsletters, so a complaint cannot affect the mailbox that sends portal notifications — needs the Exchange application access policy to include it.

Language keys 9 640 each.

## §326 v1.140.0 (R97-040) — Newsletter lists; subscribers into Contacts; contact categories

Decisions of 14/11/2026: combined approach — one register (Contacts), lists by category; existing business contacts included; categories applied to people.

Migration `install/migrate_cp_r97_040_nl_lists_contact_categories.sql`: `contacts.origin`, `contacts.needs_review`, `nl_subs.contact_id`, `nl_subs.role`, table `nl_lists` (name, note, filter JSON `{roles, cats, subs, country}`, `add_emails`, `skip_emails`). Check key `r97q`.

`app/ct.php`: `ct_categories()` gains yacht_mgr, tech_mgr, engineer, yard_mgr, project_mgr, designer, purchasing, surveyor, sales, broker, agent; `ct_cat_guess()` maps a job title (EN / EL / IT words) to a category; `ct_autocat()` applies it to people on "Other" — run once by `nf_sweep()` (setting `ct_autocat_done`), again from the Lists tab (action `nl_autocat`), and on saving a contact left on Other; saving a contact clears `needs_review`.

`app/nl.php`: `nl_list_members()` — dynamic: active contacts with an e-mail matching organisation role AND person category AND country AND subscription (each only when set; primary organisation and `contact_companies`), plus the added addresses, minus the excluded ones; `nl_audience()` takes `lists`; statistics group label = person category (`c:<key>`); `nl_sub_to_contact()` on confirmation — existing contact by e-mail gets the subscriptions, otherwise a new contact (origin website, to review, category from role / kind, organisation only by `org_find()` name match, never created). `nl.php` and `web_feed.php` now load `ct` and `org`. Views: Lists tab with form and member list; list ticks in the issue's Audience; linked contact and "to review" on the subscribers tab.

Website R17: the sign-up form has "My role" (owner, owner's representative, captain, chief engineer, ETO, technical manager, yacht manager, shipyard manager, project manager, designer, purchasing manager, surveyor, other), passed to the portal.

**Security review**: list actions behind staff check, CSRF and `nl_can()`; filter values taken from fixed lists; all statements bound; a subscriber can create one contact only after confirming the e-mail address, with text fields cut and escaped on display, and can never create or change an organisation; the role value is checked against the category list. No finding.

Language keys 9 680 each.

## §327 v1.140.1 (R97-041) — Contacts import: fill-only mode, Country column; more categories

`ct_categories()` gains `technical`, `service`, `executive`. `ct_cat_guess()` is now an ordered list of [category, words] (a category may appear twice): electrical / automation titles are caught as `technical` before the design words; purchasing, production, project, service, design, sales and executive words extended (EN / IT / NL / ES / EL); `sales` is tested before `executive` ("Sales Director" → sales); new `consultant` words.

`ct_import()`: header `Country` (also Nation, Country of work) → `companies.country` when empty; `$GLOBALS['ct_import_fill_only']` (tick `fill_only` on the import form, default on): for a matched contact only blank fields are written, the category only when it is `other`, organisation / customer only when missing, name and active flag never; values equal to the stored ones are not written, and such contacts are counted as `contacts_same`. No database step.

Worldwide contacts workbook of 09/10/2026 (1 098 people, 563 companies) converted to the template with categories from the job titles — `GAS-contacts-import_worldwide_R0_20261114.xlsx`; sandbox: dry run writes nothing; real run creates 1 085 contacts and 563 organisations with country; an existing contact with the same e-mail kept its name, title and category; a second run changes nothing.

**Security review**: the tick is read as a boolean; the Country value is cut to 80 characters and bound; the import stays behind the same staff and CSRF checks; no personal data of the workbook was sent to the assistant or stored in the repository. No finding.

Language keys 9 685 each.

## §328 v1.140.2 (R97-042) — All contacts with a valid e-mail subscribed to the four lists

**Request**: "to all the contacts with a valid email add automatically in the database the selection for Newsletter, Campaigns, Events, Greetings".

**What changed**

- `ct_default_subs($email)` (`app/ct.php`) returns the four subscription keys for a syntactically valid address that is not on the suppression list `nl_optout` (`ct_optout()`), otherwise an empty string.
- `ct_subscribe_all($dry)` sets the four subscriptions on every active contact with such an address that misses at least one; `$dry` only counts.
- One-time run in the sweep (`app/nf.php`), guarded by the setting `ct_suball_done`; the number of contacts changed is kept in `ct_suball_n`. No database step.
- Excel import: a **new** contact with an empty Subscriptions cell gets the default; an existing contact is not changed by the import (fill-only rule of §327).
- Contact form: a new contact opens with the four ticks set.
- Web Sales › Newsletters › Lists: button "Subscribe n contact(s) with an e-mail to all lists" (action `nl_suball`, gate `nl_can_send()`), shown only when n > 0.

**Not changed**: unsubscribed and bounced addresses stay excluded from every sending; a contact un-ticked by hand after the one-time run stays un-ticked unless the button is pressed again.

**Tests (sandbox)**: one page load set the four subscriptions on the contact with a valid e-mail that lacked them, left the unsubscribed address and the malformed address untouched and stored `ct_suball_done = 1`; import of the worldwide workbook R1 created 1 086 contacts, all with the four subscriptions; lint clean; sweep and API test as before.

**Security review**: the action is POST with CSRF token and limited to those who may send newsletters; addresses are validated with `FILTER_VALIDATE_EMAIL`; the suppression list is honoured before any tick is set, so an unsubscribe can not be undone by the rule; queries are bound or constant. Compliance note: the subscriptions are set by the company, not by the person — the mailings rely on legitimate interest towards business contacts, so every issue must keep the unsubscribe link (it does) and the Privacy Policy R5 wording covers it; contacts who are private persons should be un-ticked. No performance cost: one query on the Lists tab, one run in the sweep.

Language keys 9 688 each.

## §329 v1.140.3 (R97-043) — Newsletter lists: organisation type OR person category

**Report**: on Newsletters › Lists the first list showed 71 members and the three others 0 although categories were ticked.

**Cause**: `nl_list_members()` combined organisation role AND person category. "Yard" + "Shipyard manager" kept only shipyard managers whose organisation carries the role Yard; most organisations have no role yet and most people in a yard carry another category, so the result was empty.

**What changed**

- `nl_list_match($f)` — filter key `match`: `any` (default, also for lists saved before) or `all`.
- `nl_list_members()`: with both roles and categories and `any`, two selections are merged (people of the organisation types; people with the categories); `all` keeps the former behaviour. Subscription and country stay restrictions in both modes.
- Country accepts several values separated by commas (`co.country IN (…)`); the form field proposes the countries in use (`<datalist>`), new hint `nl.l_country_hint`.
- List form: select "Organisation type and person category" (Either / Both); list table shows "(either)" / "(both)" and marks the country.

**Tests (sandbox)**: yard + shipyard manager/captain → either: 5 people, both: 2; with country Italy → 2; "Italy, greece" + both → 2; one side only unchanged; page renders; lint clean; sweep and API test as before.

**Security review**: `match` is reduced to two literals; countries are bound parameters; datalist values are escaped; action unchanged (POST, CSRF, `nl_can()`). No finding. Performance: at most one extra query pair per list.

Language keys 9 693 each.

## §330 v1.140.4 (R97-044) — Portal slow: sweep ran on every page (time-zone slip); proforma `$exp` warnings

**Report**: Diagnostics › Slowest pages listed pages of 3.4–4.6 s with only 136–364 ms in the database; PHP error log full of `Undefined variable $exp` in `views/proforma.php`; "the portal became a bit slow".

**Diagnosis**

1. `nf_sweep()` is throttled to once per 60 s by comparing `nf_cursor.run_at` with the clock. The value is written by the database with `NOW()` in the database zone (`tz_db`, UTC by default) and was read with `strtotime()` in the company zone (Europe/Athens), so it always looked 2–3 hours old: **the sweep ran on every page**, before the first byte of HTML. It runs 15 notification sources, four reminder jobs, the website statistics pull, one news feed (up to 9 s time-out) and one newsletter batch (up to 15 Graph calls). Seconds outside the database with few database milliseconds is exactly this signature. Sandbox: dashboard 343 queries with the sweep, 104 without.
2. Same slip in `presence_touch()` (one `UPDATE customer_users` per request instead of per minute) and in the newsletter sign-up rule "one confirmation e-mail per ten minutes" (did not hold).
3. Projects page: `eng_alerts()` rebuilds every 3 minutes with ~15 queries per open sub-project (3 683 queries / 3.9 s in the live log).
4. `views/proforma.php` line 122: the statement defining `$exp` sat behind a `//` comment since R95-006, so the edit-payment form never received the payment.

**What changed**

- `tz_age($at)` (`app/helpers.php`): seconds since a database moment, zone-safe. Used in `nf_sweep()`, new `nf_sweep_due()`, `presence_touch()`, `nl_subscribe` confirmation rule.
- The sweep left the page: `layout_head()` only asks `nf_sweep_due()`; when due it runs `nf_sweep_detached()` after the response (PHP-FPM / LiteSpeed: shutdown function with `fastcgi_finish_request()` / `litespeed_finish_request()`), otherwise the page carries `data-bg="1"` and `portal.js` calls `POST index.php?p=bg` after load. The session is released before the work.
- `eng_alerts()` cache 600 s (still dropped at once by any engineering budget / request change).
- `proforma.php`: `$ep` / `$exp` on their own line.

**Tests (sandbox)**: with `run_at` 5 minutes old the page shows `data-bg`, `?p=bg` returns 204 and the next pages do not ask again; anonymous and cross-site calls of `?p=bg` get 403; vessels 63 → 47 queries, dashboard 343 → 104; proforma page without warnings; lint, sweep and API test clean.

**Security review**: `?p=bg` takes no parameter, needs a signed-in session, refuses cross-site calls (`Sec-Fetch-Site`), and can do nothing but start the already locked and throttled sweep — it cannot be used to force work more often than once a minute. Fixing the confirmation rule closes a way to make the portal send repeated confirmation e-mails to one address. No new inline script (the call lives in `portal.js`); CSP unchanged.

**Proposed next**: advanced diagnostics (request timing by phase, outbound-call log, background-job panel, slow-query digest with EXPLAIN, error grouping, weekly health e-mail) — offered to Nikos for decision.

Language keys 9 693 each.

## §331 v1.141.0 (R97-045) — Daily health report by e-mail

**Request**: "send to the developer (me) a health report daily at 7.00 AM, create a schedule for this in the settings".

**What changed**

- `app/diag.php`: `dh_on()`, `dh_time()`, `dh_admins()`, `dh_chosen()`, `dh_recipients()`, `dh_recent_lines()`, `dh_build()` → [subject, text], `dh_send()`, `dh_maybe()`, `dh_save_from_post()`.
- Settings in `cp_settings`: `dh_on` (default 1), `dh_time` (default 07:00, company zone), `dh_to` (administrator ids; empty = all administrators), `dh_last` (day), `dh_last_at`, `dh_last_n`. No database step.
- Sent from the background sweep (`nf_sweep()` → `dh_maybe()`), once a day at or after the time; since §330 the sweep runs after the page, so nobody waits for the diagnostics run.
- Settings › About / Versions › Diagnostics: block "Daily health report by e-mail" (on/off, time, recipients, Save, Send now, last sent). Actions `dh_save`, `dh_send_now` (administrators).
- Report: summary; needs attention; slow pages 24 h grouped per page (ordered by total waiting time); PHP log 24 h grouped by message (numbers and the installation path normalised); background sweep, newsletter queue, sign-ins; full `diag_text()`.
- Found by the first sample report and fixed: `ts.php` timesheet PDF footer closure did not import `$bd` — empty branch footer line and two warnings per page.

**Limit**: there is no server clock job; with no activity at 07:00 the report leaves at the first page view after it. A cPanel cron job calling the portal every 15 minutes would make it exact — offered.

**Tests (sandbox)**: sample report built (20 checks, grouped slow pages and log); recipients = active administrators; time before the chosen time → nothing sent; second call the same day → nothing sent; lint, sweep, API test clean.

**Security review**: recipients are restricted to active administrators by id (no free-text address), because the report contains server paths and log lines; the user column of the slow-pages log is left out; the mail is plain text; actions are POST + CSRF + administrator only; time is validated by pattern. E-mail is not an encrypted store — the report carries no passwords, tokens or personal data, only technical state.

Language keys 9 704 each (the figure 9 693 in §329–§330 should read 9 694).

## §332 v1.142.0 (R97-046) — Advanced diagnostics: System health

**Request**: "implement 1-6" of the proposal — timing by phase, calls to other servers, background jobs, slow statements, error grouping, 30-day trend.

**New module** `app/perf.php` (loaded by `cache.php`, so every entry point has it). All data in small files under `storage/perf/` — no database step.

| # | What | Functions | File |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Phases of a slow page (start / data / draw, database, other servers) | `perf_mark()` (`route` in `index.php`, `head` in `layout_head()`), `perf_phases()` | 8th field of `storage/slow-pages.log` |
| 2 | Calls to other servers | `perf_out($ch, $what)` after every `curl_exec()` (mailer ×5, webstats, mi, ai, softone, vm, rcr), `perf_out_summary()` | `outbound.log` |
| 3 | Background jobs | `nf_job()` around each job of `nf_sweep()`, `perf_jobs_save()`, `perf_jobs()` | `jobs.json` |
| 4 | Slow statements (> 100 ms, max 10 per request) | `perf_slow_query()` from `db_meter()`, `perf_sql_digest()`, `perf_sql_explain()` | `slow-queries.log` |
| 5 | Errors grouped | `perf_err_handler()` (adds `{p=page v=version}`, max 40 lines per request), `perf_err_shutdown()`, `perf_release_note()`, `perf_errors()` | `storage/php-errors.log` |
| 6 | Trend | `perf_finish()` from `db_meter_finish()`, `perf_rollup()` (daily, in `hk_tick()`), `perf_trend()` | `<day>.log` → `days.json` (90 days) |

- Page `views/admin_health.php` (`?p=admin_health`, administrators), menu Settings › System › System health, button on the Diagnostics card. Action `perf_clear` (out | sql | jobs | trend).
- `nf_sweep()` stamps `run_at` at its start as well, so a long sweep is not requested again by the next page.
- Daily health report: yesterday's typical / slow 5 % against the days before (marked SLOWER at +50 %), new error kinds since the release, failing calls, failing jobs; subject shows the number of new errors.

**Tests (sandbox)**: 30 days of synthetic timings → chart and tables; real failing call logged with host only (path and query absent); statement with an e-mail literal logged as `'?'`; digest groups 6 variants as one; EXPLAIN of a SELECT returns the plan, of an UPDATE is refused; 60 identical warnings in one request → 40 lines, group shows page `offers`, version, "New"; jobs file filled by a sweep; six tabs render without warnings; lint, sweep and API test clean.

**Security review**
- Page and action administrators only; action POST + CSRF; `what`, `tab`, `pg`, `x` reduced to fixed lists / character classes.
- EXPLAIN runs only on a statement found in the portal's own log by hash — never on text from the browser — and only when it is a single SELECT; EXPLAIN does not execute the statement.
- Nothing personal is stored: no user, no parameter values (literals replaced), no URL path or query of a call (host only).
- `storage/` is closed to the web; the files are size-capped (1.5–3 MB, trimmed in place) and the raw day files are removed after the roll-up.
- The per-request cap on logged warnings stops one broken loop from filling the disk — the proforma warning had written 440 lines in a week.
- Cost per page: one file append of about 40 bytes; nothing else unless a call, a slow statement or a warning occurs.

Language keys 9 812 each.

## §333 v1.142.1 (R97-047) — Exports of contacts and customers: CEO, General Manager, Developer only

**Request**: "Export of contacts and customer will be only allowed to CEO, developer and General Manager".

**What changed**

- `export_people_can()` (`app/col.php`): staff account whose Golden A/S role name is `CEO`, `General Manager` or `Developer` (case-insensitive). `require_export_people($what)` answers 403 with `ex.only_mgmt` otherwise.
- Applied to `ex_contacts()` (`?p=contacts_export`), `ex_companies()` (`companies_export`), `ex_customers()` (`customers_export`), `ex_users()` (`users_export` — it lists the customers' people too) and the unused `ct_export_csv()`. Before: any portal administrator.
- Export buttons on Contacts, Organisations, Customers, Accounts and Settings › Import are shown only when `export_people_can()`.
- `export_log()` → `storage/exports.log`: time, user, list, rows, ok / refused. The daily health report counts exports and refused attempts of the last 24 hours and lists them.
- No database step. Vessels, projects, equipment, planning, HR and finance exports keep their own rules.

**Tests (sandbox)**: CEO → four exports 200; administrator with role Technical Manager → 403 on all four, no buttons; project manager → 403; log shows 4 ok + 5 refused; lint, sweep, API test clean.

**Security review**: this closes the bulk-extraction path for the customer and contact base for every account except three roles; the server refuses the request itself, not only the button; attempts are logged, so a try by a compromised administrator account becomes visible the next morning. Not covered by this rule and worth knowing: a person who may open the Contacts list can still read it on screen page by page, and the mobile app service returns contacts to staff — a per-day view limit or an alert on unusual reading volume can be added if wanted.

Language keys 9 813 each.

## §334 v1.143.0 (R97-048) — System Budgets: consistency checks 1–10

**Request**: "propose ways to check budget consistency" → "Add all 1-10".

**New module** `app/sbc.php` (loaded after `sb.php`), no database step. `sbc_run($B, $only)` returns findings `['c','sev','code','msg','at']`, counts and extra tables.

| # | Check | Function | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Arithmetic: stored totals ≠ lines, negatives, discount range, hours without rate, flat / cost rate zero, expense mark-up drift, commission range | `sbc_c1` | block |
| 2 | Completeness: no sub-project, empty / zero lines, no section / discipline, unpriced, duplicates, description empty or naming work with no hours | `sbc_c2` | warn (unpriced = block) |
| 3 | Masters: labour rate vs rate card of the zone, part cost vs inventory (lowest price → last price → average), price age, inactive items, free parts that match an inventory code | `sbc_c3` | warn |
| 4 | Profit: line / section / group / total vs `sb_min_profit()`; below cost | `sbc_c4` | block below cost, warn below minimum |
| 5 | Ratios (from 40 h): PM share, commissioning, engineering, overtime, travel and allowance outside Attica, FAT, documentation, logistics | `sbc_c5` | warn / info |
| 6 | Similar budgets: sale per hour, material / labour cost, profit, engineering share, other costs share against the 10th–90th percentile of finished / converted budgets (same template when five exist) | `sbc_c6`, `sbc_population()` (4 statements, cached 15 min) | info |
| 7 | Revision diff by code + description, each side priced with its own header | `sbc_c7`, `sbc_diff()` | info; warn on profit drop |
| 8 | Offer: exists, total equals budget sale (with or without commission), edited / revised after conversion, sub-project budget revision, hours not fed | `sbc_c8` | warn |
| 9 | Actuals of the sub-project (`bg_calc()`): discipline over budget, unbudgeted hours, parts over, forecast overrun | `sbc_c9` | warn |
| 10 | AI review of scope against lines, on request, stored in `storage/sbc/ai-<id>.json` | `sbc_ai_review()`, `sbc_c10` | info |

- Gate: `sb_finish_blockers()` and `sb_convert()` include `sbc_blocking()` (blocking findings of checks 1, 2, 4). `sbc_override()` — management (`sb_can_reopen()`), reason ≥ 10 characters, `sb_events` kind `override` for the current revision.
- Pages: card on the budget (`views/_sb_checks.php`), `?p=system_budget_checks` overview (`sbc_overview()`, summary per budget cached 10 min) with the limits form.
- Actions: `sbc_recalc`, `sbc_override`, `sbc_ai`, `sbc_settings`. Settings `sbc_price_tol` 5, `sbc_price_age` 6, `sbc_profit_drop` 5, `sbc_pm_share` 3, `sbc_ot_share` 25, `sbc_overrun` 10.

**Tests (sandbox)**: nine budgets opened — findings match the data (rate drift listed per line, below-cost line blocks, revision table R1 → R2); Finish refused with the finding text; reason of 5 characters refused; management acceptance recorded; project manager cannot accept; stored total altered by hand → button appears, recalculation clears it; overview 191 statements cold, 45 cached; no PHP warnings. AI review not exercised (no key in the sandbox).

**Security review**: read-only module; actions POST + CSRF behind `sb_guard()`; acceptance management only and recorded with name, time, address; AI review sends titles, descriptions, codes, quantities and hours — no prices, no customer or personal data — and follows the existing AI limits and log; the stored answer is stripped of markup. Statement placeholders throughout; IN-lists built from integers or bound values.

Language keys 9 997 each.

## §335 v1.144.0 (R97-049) — System Budgets: procurement step

**Request**: "the procurement should have access to the System budget in the process so to fill prices and delivery time (there should be a new step to send to procurement for pricing and delivery times and alternative parts) and then to be processed in the finished stage".

**Migration** `install/migrate_cp_r97_049_sb_procurement.sql` (key `r97r`): `sb_budgets.proc_state` (NULL · requested · done), `proc_note`, `proc_req_by/at`, `proc_done_by/at`; `sb_parts.lead_weeks`, `proc_cost`, `proc_supplier`, `proc_note`, `alt_descr`, `alt_cost`, `alt_lead_weeks`, `proc_by`, `proc_at`.

**Module** `app/sbp.php` (all guarded by `sbp_has()`):

- `sbp_request()` engineer → procurement (notification `sb:proc` to accounts with `can_procurement`); `sbp_recall()`.
- `sbp_can()` = procurement accounts (`pr_admin()`), management, administrators. `sbp_get()` returns identity + part lines only.
- `sbp_save($id, $confirmAll)`: cost becomes `unit_cost` and `proc_cost`, origin "Procurement · name · date · supplier", `proc_at` stamped for lines procurement typed or worked on; on return every posted price is confirmed. Only while `requested` and the budget is draft / working.
- `sbp_return()` → `done`, notification to requester / owner / creator. `sbp_use_alt()` swaps part and alternative.
- `sbp_blockers()` in `sb_finish_blockers()`: waiting at procurement; or (rule `sb_proc_required`, default on) part lines with quantity and no `proc_at`.
- Part lines locked for the engineer while `requested` (`sb_save_parts_from_post`, `sb_add_inv_part`).
- Revisions keep the procurement columns (`SBP_PART_COLS` merged into the snapshot); a copy / template drops them, so a copy is priced again.
- `sb_convert()`: longest `lead_weeks` → `offers.delivery_parts` when empty ("n weeks from order").
- Consistency check 2: at procurement, lines to send, lines added after return, priced lines without delivery time, cost changed after procurement; check 3 skips the inventory comparison for lines freshly priced by procurement.

**Pages**: `?p=system_budgets_proc` (queue, Procurement menu with the number waiting), `?p=system_budget_proc&id=` (pricing form), card `views/_sb_proc.php` on the budget, pill in header and list, switch on the Consistency overview. Actions `sbp_request`, `sbp_recall`, `sbp_save` (+ `return`), `sbp_use_alt`, `sbp_required`.

**Tests (sandbox)**: Finish refused before sending; send → notification to the procurement account; engineer cannot save parts while at procurement; procurement-only account: budget and budget list 403, queue and form open, 124 inputs, no sale / margin text on the form; prices, delivery, supplier, alternative stored and totals recalculated; unknown line id ignored; a budget not at procurement refused; plain Save confirms only touched lines, Return confirms the rest; save after return refused; alternative swap; project manager without the procurement right gets 403 on the form; no PHP warnings.

**Security review**: the pricing form is a separate read model — a procurement account never receives labour, sale prices, margins or totals, and cannot reach the budget pages; writes are limited to nine columns of lines that belong to the budget in the URL, only while the budget is at procurement; actions POST + CSRF; texts trimmed and length-capped, numbers clamped; every step in the budget history with name and address. Note: a procurement account sees the project code, vessel name and title of the budget — needed to quote.

Language keys 10 071 each.

## §336 v1.145.0 (R97-050) — KYMA website ↔ portal (two sites)

**Request**: specification `GAS-KYM-SPC-PORTAL-001` R0, "For implementation": KYMA enquiries, statistics and newsletter visible in the portal, separate from Golden; contacts marked by brand. Nothing changes in the KYMA repository; Golden behaviour unchanged.

**Migration** `install/migrate_cp_r97_050_kyma_site.sql` (key `r97s`, idempotent): `site` on `web_inquiries` (+ `contact_id`, index `ix_web_site`), `web_stats_day` (PK `site,d,dim,k`), `web_stats_hour` (PK `site,d,h`), `nl_subs` (unique `site,email`), `nl_optout` (PK `site,email`), `nl_issues`; `contacts.brands`, `companies.brands` `SET('golden','kyma')` default `golden`. Existing rows become `golden`. Everything is guarded by `kym_has()` until the step has run.

**Site resolution** (`web_feed.php`): the key decides the site — `web_site_key` → golden, `web_site_key_kyma` → kyma, both compared with `hash_equals`, an empty stored key never matches. `X-Site` header and body `site` must agree with the key (403 otherwise). The KYMA key opens only `r=contact`, `r=subscribe`, `r=unsubscribe`; ticket, access and the GET counts stay Golden-only (403).

**Enquiries** (`app/web.php`, `views/web_inbox.php`): `web_ref($site)` → `KYM-YYYY-NNNN` / `WEB-…`; duplicate check per site; notification prefix "KYMA · "; tabs per site with counters, topic filter; `web_inquiry_to_contact()` (action `web_inq_contact`) creates or links a contact with brand, origin `kyma_website`, optional organisation; careers: `kym_careers_can()` (administrators, management) for list, detail and counters, notified to management only, never a contact; `web_purge($site,$months,$careersOnly)` (action `web_purge`, administrators).

**Statistics** (`app/webstats.php`, `views/web_stats.php`): current site via `ws_use()`; settings of KYMA carry `_kyma` (`ws_feed_url_kyma`, `ws_feed_key_kyma`); `ws_sweep()` pulls each configured site with its own throttle and try / catch; all readers filter by site; two cards and a per-site breakdown; the weekly digest has a KYMA section.

**Newsletter** (`app/nl.php`, `nl.php`, views): issue, subscriber and opt-out rows carry the site; `nl_audience()` is limited to the site of the issue (own subscribers, contacts carrying the brand, own opt-out list); `nl_brand()` gives name, colours, logo (`assets/kyma-logo-white.png` when present, otherwise a text mark), home and privacy links; sender `nl_from_kyma` — no fall-back to the Golden mailbox (`nl_ready('kyma')`; sign-up answers 503 until set); confirmation mail in eight languages; public pages branded by the token's site; `r=unsubscribe` by address answers `{"status":"ok"}` in a constant 0.25 s whether or not the address exists; `List-Unsubscribe` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post` through Graph MIME send (on for KYMA, off for Golden by default), with automatic return to the normal send for an hour if Graph refuses.

**Contacts** (`app/ct.php`, `app/export.php`, views): brands on edit forms, filter `brand`, pills, Brands column in exports, list filter `brand`; leaving the Golden list does not change KYMA and the reverse.

**Settings**: Settings › Website › KYMA website (action `web_kyma_key` rotate / disable — the key is generated on the server, stored, and shown once), KYMA statistics feed; Newsletters › Settings › KYMA sender.

**Tests (sandbox)** — the twelve acceptance tests of the specification: 1 Golden contact → `WEB-` site golden; 2 KYMA contact → `KYM-2026-0001`, notification "KYMA · "; 3 key and declared site disagree → 403 both ways; 4 KYMA key on ticket and counts → 403; 5 careers stored, management notified, hidden from a project manager (list, direct link, counter), refused as contact; 6 KYMA sign-up → pending, KYMA confirmation page, contact and organisation marked KYMA; 7 KYMA unsubscribe leaves the Golden subscription; 8 unsubscribe answers identical in content and time; 9 KYMA audience contains no Golden-only address; 10 one feed failing does not stop the other; 11 figures per site; 12 brand filter. Also: no KYMA key configured → an empty or any other key is refused (403); purge by a non-administrator 403; key shown once; pages sweep and app API test unchanged. **Not testable in the sandbox**: real sending through Microsoft Graph (KYMA mailbox, MIME send with list headers) and the live KYMA statistics feed.

**Security review**: site taken from the key, never from the request; constant-time comparison, keys never logged, shown once, one per site so one can be rotated without the other; KYMA key cannot reach tickets, access requests or counts; career data limited to administrators and management and removable by age; unsubscribe gives no hint whether an address is known; no cross-site sending; all new actions POST + CSRF with role checks; new columns read through placeholders; no inline script. Proposed next (no cost in speed): rotate the Golden site key too so both keys date from this release; an expiry reminder for site keys older than 12 months in the daily health report; SPF / DKIM / DMARC records for the KYMA sender domain before the first issue.

Language keys 10 135 each.

## §337 v1.146.0 (R97-051) — Newsletters: approval by the CEO / General Manager

**Request**: "do we have an approval process for the Newsletters from the CEO or General Manager before launched?" → "do as you say and CEO and General Manager dont need approval".

**Migration** `install/migrate_cp_r97_051_nl_approval.sql` (key `r97t`): `nl_issues.appr_state` (NULL · pending · approved · returned), `appr_req_by/name/at`, `appr_note`, `appr_by/name/at`, `appr_reply`, `appr_hash`, `appr_n`. Guarded by `nl_appr_has()`.

**Logic** (`app/nl.php`):

- `nl_approver()` = Owner scope, CEO, General Manager / Managing Director (`mgmt_can()` or role name `ceo`). They approve and need no approval.
- `nl_appr_on()` = setting `nl_approval` (default 1) and at least one active approver account (`nl_appr_ids()`), so sending can never be locked out.
- `nl_hash()` = SHA-1 over subject, preheader, language, blocks, audience, site. `nl_appr_state()` treats an approval whose hash no longer matches as absent; `nl_appr_recheck()` clears it after a save.
- `nl_appr_request()` (author; content and recipients required; notification `nl:appr` to the approvers), `nl_appr_withdraw()` (author or sender), `nl_appr_decide()` (approver only; comment required on return; notification to the author).
- `nl_editable()` refuses while `pending`. `nl_queue()`: `nl_may_send()` (administrators and management as before, plus the author once approved) and `nl_appr_ok()`.
- Actions `nl_appr_request`, `nl_appr_withdraw`, `nl_appr_approve`, `nl_appr_approve_send`, `nl_appr_return`; `nl_settings` stores the switch only for an approver.

**Pages**: card `#approval` on the issue, state pill on the issue and in the list, switch on Newsletters › Settings.

**Tests (sandbox)**: page before the migration unchanged; author without approval cannot send (message names the rule); author cannot approve; submit → pending, approvers notified, save refused, Save button gone; return without comment refused, with comment → author sees it; resubmit → approve → author may send with the form unchanged; change of subject after approval → approval cancelled and send refused; approved state with a foreign fingerprint written straight into the database → refused; CEO sends directly; withdraw; switch not changeable by a non-approver, off → card gone, on again. Page sweep unchanged. Real sending is not possible in the sandbox (no mail connection).

**Security review**: the gate is enforced in `nl_queue()` on the server, not by the buttons; approval is bound to the content and audience by fingerprint and compared in constant time; approver identity from the staff role, never from the request; all actions POST + CSRF; comments trimmed and length-capped, shown escaped; the switch is limited to the approvers. Proposed next (no cost in speed): list approvals and sends in the daily health report; a second approver for audiences above a set size.

Language keys 10 173 each.

## §338 v1.147.0 (R97-052) — Organisations: known shipyards → Yard role, on approval

**Request**: "check all known Shipyards in europe, US and worldwide and just add the yard tick if the name or part of the name is matching the Company or Organization name" — "ask me to approve the list before any changes". The name list and the rules were shown and approved on 15/11/2026.

**Module** `app/yards.php` (no migration):

- `YD_STEMS` (found anywhere in the name: shipyard, shipbuild, ship repair, dockyard, drydock, boatyard, werft, scheepswerf, cantiere / cantieri, chantier nav, chantiers, astillero, estaleiro, tersane, ναυπηγ, stocznia, brodogradili, skipsverft …), `YD_WORDS` (whole words: yard, yards, werf, verft, varv), `YD_NAMES` (about 270 known yards by region, whole word / phrase), `YD_WEAK` (services, components, trading … → proposed but not ticked).
- `yd_norm()` lower case, accents folded (Latin and Greek tonos), `&` / `+` → "and", punctuation to spaces. `yd_match()` returns the longest known name, otherwise the first general word.
- `yd_scan()` read-only: organisations with `kind <> 'yard'` and no `yard` in `roles` whose name matches.
- `yd_apply($ids)`: re-runs the scan and changes only ids that are proposed now; `roles` gets `yard` (the placeholder `other` is dropped), `kind` = `co_main_kind()`; one line per run in `storage/yards.log` (who, how many, which names).
- `yd_can()` = `ct_manage()` without the assistant scope.

**Page** `?p=companies_yards` (button on Organisations), action `yd_apply`.

**Tests (sandbox)**: 37 sample names through the matcher (Lürssen with and without umlaut, Blohm+Voss, Abeking & Rasmussen, Greek ναυπηγεία, Oderwerft, 3. Maj; no match for Vineyard, Yardley, Balkan, Christ, Oceania, Admiral Hotel, Viking Line, Riva del Garda); page lists only matches, group company unticked; project manager: page 403, action refused, no button; apply with one matching, one matching with other roles and one non-matching id → 2 changed, roles kept, non-matching id ignored; second visit shows only what is left. Page sweep unchanged.

**Role “Other”** ("other will be ticked only if no other role is ticked"): `co_roles_tidy()` drops `other` whenever another role is present — used by `co_roles()` (display, merges, the yard step) and by `co_save_from_post()`; `co_tidy_other()` applies the same to stored rows (roles appended by supplier / customer links and imports) from the background sweep, at most once an hour (`co_tidy_at`), which also corrects the existing rows after this release. Tests: stored `other,customer` → `customer` after the sweep; save with Other + Yard → `yard`; nothing ticked → `other`.

**Security review**: read-only scan; the change is POST + CSRF, administrators only, and limited on the server to rows the scan proposes at that moment, so a forged id cannot mark an arbitrary organisation; one column pair written through placeholders; every run logged; no personal data leaves the portal — the yard names are a fixed list in the code. Proposed next (no cost in speed): show the runs of `yards.log` and `exports.log` on System health.

Language keys 10 195 each.

## §339 v1.148.0 (R97-053) — Sub-projects: automatic order

**Request**: "in the sub projects provide me the same automatic numbering for the order also since it makes a mess and every time i have to check the correct order manually".

**Before**: `subprojects.sort_order` was typed by hand (default 0) — doubles, gaps and zeros; lists sort by `sort_order, code`.

**Now** (`app/pm_actions.php`):

- `sp_reorder($pid, $movedId = 0, $pos = 0)`: reads the sub-projects in their present order (numbered first, then the unnumbered by code), places `$movedId` at `$pos` (0 or beyond the end = last) and writes 1…N; only changed rows are updated.
- `sp_next_order($pid)` = count + 1, shown in the form.
- Called from `subproject_save`, `subproject_update`, the Excel import (insert and update with an order), and after a delete.
- `sp_reorder_all()`: one pass over the projects whose order is not exactly 1…N, run once from the background sweep (`sp_order_v1`).
- Form (`views/project_new.php`): the field shows the next number / the present position, `min 1`, `max N`, with a hint. Keys `sp.order_hint_new`, `sp.order_hint`.

**Tests (sandbox)**: project with five sub-projects all on 0 → new one gets 6 and the five become 1–5 by code; new at position 2 → the rest shift; move to 1; move beyond the end → last; one-off pass → no project out of order, display order unchanged.

**Security review**: no new entry point; the position is cast to an integer and clamped by the routine, updates use placeholders and are limited to the sub-projects of the one project; the existing staff check and CSRF of the actions apply. Nothing to propose for this change.

Language keys 10 197 each.

## §340 v1.149.0 (R97-054) — KYMA mark by role

**Request**: "All contacts, companies organizations with email marked as customers and yards, owners, engineering office please tick KYMA contact as well automatically".

**Rule** (`app/helpers.php`): `KYM_AUTO_ROLES` = customer, yard, owner, consultant (organisation roles; consultant / designer stands for the engineering office, there is no separate organisation role); `KYM_AUTO_CATS` = owner, owner_rep, yard, yard_mgr, technical, designer (contact categories; `technical` = "Engineering / technical office").

- Organisation: `customer_id` set or one of the roles (in `roles` or as `kind`), and an e-mail on the organisation or on one of its contacts.
- Contact: valid-looking e-mail, and `customer_id` set, or one of the categories, or its organisation carries one of the roles.
- `kym_auto_where()` builds both conditions once; `kym_auto_count()` (read-only) and `kym_auto_mark()` (adds `kyma` to `brands` with `CONCAT_WS`, never removes anything; line in `storage/kyma-mark.log`).
- `kym_auto_on()` = setting `kym_auto` (default 1). Background job `kyma-mark` at most once an hour (`kym_auto_at`); the first run after the release marks the existing records.
- Settings › Website › KYMA website: card with the count waiting, the switch and "Save and mark now" — action `kym_auto_save` (administrators).

**Effect on newsletters**: a KYMA issue with "contacts" in its audience now also reaches these contacts, subject to their subscriptions and the KYMA opt-out list as before.

**Tests (sandbox)**: count before = rows changed; contacts of an owner / customer organisation marked whatever their category; supplier-only contact with e-mail not marked; nobody without e-mail marked; Golden mark kept on every row; project manager cannot run it; switch off and on; second run changes nothing.

**Security and data protection review**: administrators only, POST + CSRF; fixed role and category lists compiled into the SQL (no request data in it); only one column touched; logged. Data protection: the mark makes people reachable by a second sender (KYMA) who gave their address to Golden A/S — the KYMA issues must carry the KYMA identity, a working unsubscribe (in place since v1.145.0) and be relevant to their business role; the approval step of v1.146.0 applies. Proposed (no cost in speed): state in the privacy notice of both sites that Golden A/S and KYMA Marine Power share business contacts for their newsletters.

Language keys 10 205 each.

## §341 v1.150.0 (R97-055) — Sales pipeline: branch filter

**Request**: "Create Branch filter to Sales Pipeline".

- `app/pipe.php`: `pp_open($f)` accepts `branch` — `COALESCE(s.branch_id, p.branch_id, <default branch>) = ?` with a join to the sub-project (same rule as `of_list()`, R96-002) and returns `branch_id`; `pp_funnel($year, $branch = 0)` filters the same way.
- `views/pipeline.php`: `$brOn` (more than one active branch), select `branch` (`br_options()`), unknown ids fall back to all, branch code on each card, the value travels with the board / list buttons and the year form.
- No new language keys (`br.one`, `flt.any` exist), no migration.

**Tests (sandbox)**: page with branch 0 / head office / second branch / unknown id in board and list view — no errors, the select keeps the choice; three sent offers in a project moved to the second branch → 3 with that branch, 0 with the head office, 3 without filter; year form keeps the branch.

**Security review**: the branch id is cast to an integer, checked against the active branches and bound as a parameter; the existing project scope (`scope_sql`) still applies before the filter, so the filter can only narrow what the account may already see. Nothing to propose for this change.

Language keys 10 205 each (unchanged).

## §342 v1.150.1 (R97-056) — Dashboard speed; refused connections per hour

**Trigger**: daily health report of 16/11/2026 (v1.147.0): dashboard 4 slow loads in 24 h, 2.4–2.9 s, 4 200–4 400 statements, almost all in "draw"; warning "Aborted connections: 833"; News feed marinelog.com HTTP 403.

**Finding 1 — dashboard**: `kpi_cockpit()` → `kpi_projects()` → `kpi_project()` → `kpi_sub()` → `bg_calc()` costs about 11 statements per active sub-project (sandbox: 13 sub-projects = 165 statements; live ≈ 380 sub-projects ≈ 4 200). It was cached for 10 minutes, so the first visitor after every expiry paid the full cost — exactly the 4–6 slow loads a day.

**Change** (`app/kpi.php`, `app/nf.php`):

- `kpi_cockpit()` returns the kept value whenever one exists (kept for a day, with its time); only when there is none at all (after a release or a cache restart) it computes once.
- `kpi_cockpit_refresh()` in the background sweep (job `kpi-cockpit`) renews the value when it is older than 10 minutes.
- `kpi_cockpit_key()`: one value per branch scope (`br_scope_sql`) — before, the key was shared, so a member of staff limited to a branch could be shown the list computed for an unrestricted account, and the reverse.

**Measured (sandbox)**: dashboard cold 334 statements (165 KPI), with a kept value 106 (0 KPI), also with a value one hour old; the sweep then spends the 178 KPI statements and the value is fresh.

**Finding 2 — "Aborted connections 833"**: `Aborted_connects` is a counter of the whole database server (shared hosting) since its restart: refused sign-ins of every account. 833 in 28 h is about 30 an hour, with no PHP error on our side. `app/diag.php` now shows total and rate and warns only above 300 per hour (`dg.aborted`, `dg.aborted_v`, `dg.fix_aborted` reworded).

**Finding 3 — News feed 403**: marinelog.com refuses the portal's news reader, which identifies itself honestly. Not changed — the reader does not pretend to be a browser. The feed can be switched off in Marine intelligence › News sources; the other feeds are unaffected.

**Security review**: the per-scope key closes a small information leak between branch scopes on the dashboard block. No new entry point. Proposed next (speed, no security cost): load the budget figures of all sub-projects of the KPI page in a few grouped statements instead of per sub-project, so the KPI page and the sweep become light as well.

Language keys 10 206 each.

## §343 v1.151.0 (R97-057) — Customer signs on the engineer's device; signature boxes

**Request**: "In the Service reports and wherever a signature needs to be placed by the customer we need a process to prepare a preview for the customer to sign on the mobile. Also the signature name etc. keep justified in the box. Also can you propose an email approval process for the customer if they dont want to connect to the portal?"

**Before**: a service report (`fsr`) could be accepted only by a signed-in customer account (portal page or customer app) or by an administrator "on behalf"; a function check (`fc`) could carry the customer's signature only at the moment the engineer submitted it. In the PDF the name, date and address were one centred line that could run over the box.

**Migration** `install/migrate_cp_r97_057_sign_on_site.sql` (key `r97u`): `accepted_via`, `accepted_role` on `fsr_reports` and `fc_reports`. Guard `sgn_has()`.

**Module** `app/sgn.php`: `sgn_load($doc, $id)` (staff, document in scope, not an external report), `sgn_can()` (status `submitted`), `sgn_button()`, `sgn_via_label()`, `sgn_decide()` — name (3+ characters), capacity, remark; accept needs the "read" tick and a valid drawn PNG (stored with the document's own `*_store_signature`); writes `accepted_by` = the member of staff, `accepted_name` = the signer, `accepted_role`, `accepted_via = 'on_site'`, time and address, an event "signed on site by …, on the device of …", and regenerates the documents; "Do not accept" needs a remark and sets `disputed`. The update carries `AND status = 'submitted'`.

**Page** `views/sign_onsite.php` (`?p=sign_onsite&doc=fsr|fc&id=`): its own page without the portal menu (the customer holding the device has nothing to navigate to), phone first (16 px inputs, large buttons, sticky header), own small script with nonce for the pad and the checks, result screen with the signed PDF. Styles `.sgn-*` in `assets/portal.css`. Buttons on `fsr_view.php` and `fc_view.php`.

**Documents**: `PdfWriter::fitText()` (size reduced step by step, then ellipsis); `fsr_sign_parts()` gives name / capacity / date as separate lines; the boxes of the service report and of the function check use it; sub-line "Signed on site / Υπογραφή επί τόπου" (`sgn_via_label`, also prepared for "Accepted by e-mail").

**Tests (sandbox)**: page before the migration (no button, notice); empty name, missing tick, missing or invalid signature refused on the server; accept with a 55-character name and a 62-character capacity → accepted, signature stored, event, PDF rendered and inspected (everything inside the box, three lines); second attempt refused; "Do not accept" without / with remark; signed-out and customer sessions cannot use the action; phone emulation (iPhone 13): messages, drawing, accept, no sideways scroll, no script errors. Page sweep and app API test unchanged.

**Not covered**: the three native apps keep their own signing screens — the new view runs in the phone's browser inside the portal; inspection reports (SF-GLD-020) have no electronic acceptance at all (printed signature lines).

**Security review**: staff only, POST + CSRF, document must be in the account's project scope; the signature is validated as a PNG of limited size; the signer's identity is a typed name — the record therefore keeps the member of staff who presented the device, time and address, so the acceptance is attributable; no portal navigation in the signing view while the device is in the customer's hands (the session is still the engineer's: the engineer should stay present — stated in the manual). Proposed: an optional one-time code sent to the customer's e-mail or phone and typed on the device, for customers who want stronger proof than a drawn signature.

**Proposal made in the reply (not built)**: acceptance by e-mail link for customers without a portal account.

Language keys 10 249 each.

## §344 v1.152.0 (R97-058) — Acceptance by e-mail link

**Decision of the owner (16/11/2026)**: proposal accepted. Documents: service reports and offers, and whatever needs an owner approval, such as drawings. Keep the 6-digit code. Validity: service reports 24 h; the others 14 days or the validity of the offer.

**Migration** `install/migrate_cp_r97_058_doc_links.sql` (key `r97v`): table `doc_links` — document kind and id, number and title at the time of sending, `token_hash` (SHA-256, unique), recipient, language, sender, `expires_at`, code (`code_hash`, expiry, sends, tries), `fails`, `verified_at`, `pass_hash`, answer (`used_at`, `used_ip`, `decision`, signer), cancellation, reminders.

**Module** `app/dl.php`:

- `DL_DOCS` = fsr, fc, offer, oa. `dl_info()` gives every kind one shape (number, title, open?, file, customer, validity, back link).
- `dl_ttl()`: fsr 24 h; offer until `valid_until` 23:59 when that is more than an hour away, else 14 days; others 14 days.
- `dl_create()` (staff, document in scope and open, file present, mail configured, at most 10 links per document per hour): cancels an open link to the same address, stores only the hash of a 40-character token, mails the link with the file attached (plain text, portal mailbox, Reply-To the sender); if the mail fails nothing is kept.
- Public side: `dl_find()` (state on the database clock), `dl_run_as()` (the page reads and regenerates the document as the sender, in memory only — no session, no cookie of the portal), `dl_code_issue()` (6 digits, 15 minutes, one per 45 s, 6 per link), `dl_code_check()` (5 tries per code, 15 failures close the link, constant-time compare; success gives a random pass kept as an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie scoped to `sign.php`, valid 60 minutes), `dl_decide()` (own guarded updates: fsr / fc → `accepted_via = 'email'` with the drawn signature, or disputed; offer → approved / rejected; owner approval → approved / approved with comments / rejected; events in the document's own log with the signer's name and the address), `dl_after()` (notification and mail to the sender, confirmation to the recipient with the signed report).
- `dl_sweep()` in the background sweep: reminders (fsr once after 8 h; others after 3 and 7 days) — each with a new token, the old one dies.

**Public page** `customer/sign.php?t=`: loads the portal's modules (list read from `index.php`), strict headers (CSP with nonce, no framing, no referrer, no store, noindex), 60 requests per address per minute, generic answers for unknown / dead links, file only after the code (`&f=1`, sandboxed). Preview shared with the on-site view (`views/_sgn_preview.php`).

**Staff card** `views/_dl_card.php` on `fsr_view`, `fc_view`, `offer`, `approval`; actions `dl_send`, `dl_cancel`.

**Tests (sandbox, no outgoing mail there — links and codes were issued from the command line)**: pages before / after the migration; sending without mail set up refused and nothing stored; step 1 shows no document; file and decision refused without the code; wrong code, code reuse, five wrong codes, lock after 15; code confirmed in another browser not valid; accept a service report with signature → status, event, notification, PDF sub-line "Accepted by e-mail"; second decision impossible; offer with validity date (link 5.3 days) approved, offer without (14 days) rejected with reason; drawing approved with comments; link replaced, cancelled, expired; confirmation older than 60 minutes; reminder issues a new token; document decided in the portal closes the link; rate limit; junk tokens; phone emulation of the whole flow. **Not tested**: real delivery of the four kinds of mail and their attachments (Microsoft 365).

**Security review**: the link alone shows nothing — the document appears only after a code delivered to the mailbox; token never stored or logged, 40 random characters, in the address only (never in a form body); codes and passes stored as hashes, limited in time, tries and number; answers do not reveal whether a token ever existed; every decision is a guarded update on the pending status, so a link cannot change a decided document; staff cannot read the token (not even the sender); mails are plain text; the public page runs with the sender's reading rights only inside the functions it calls and has no portal session. Residual risk, by design: whoever controls the mailbox can accept — the same assurance as a signed e-mail reply, with a better record. Proposed (no cost in speed): SPF, DKIM and DMARC alignment check of the sending mailbox so the acceptance mails are not marked as spam; list of open acceptance links on System health.

Language keys 10 338 each.

## §345 v1.152.1 (R97-059) — Acceptance by e-mail: problems shown

**Request** (after a first live send that showed "Sent" but did not arrive): "if there is an error in the acceptance by email log it should be shown for the user to take action".

**What "Sent" means**: Microsoft 365 accepted the message (HTTP 202). What happens after that — wrong address, refusal, spam — was invisible in the portal.

**Migration** `install/migrate_cp_r97_059_doc_links_errors.sql` (key `r97w`): `doc_links.mail_error`, `mail_error_at`, `bounced_at`, `bounce_reason`, `expiry_told`. Guard `dl_err_has()`.

**Changes**:

- `cp_graph_send()` keeps the reason of a refusal in `$GLOBALS['cp_graph_last_error']`; `dl_mail_reason()` reads it.
- `dl_mail_result($L, $step, $ok)`: step `link` / `code` / `reminder` / `confirmation`; a failure is stored as "step: reason" and the sender is notified once per step; a later success of the same step clears it.
- `dl_bounce_check()` (in `dl_sweep`, every 10 minutes): reads the last 50 messages of three days from the inbox of the portal's mailbox (Graph, the permission already used for drafts; skipped silently when not allowed) and `dl_bounce_match()` picks those from a mail system (postmaster, mailer-daemon, Microsoft Exchange / Outlook) that name the address of an open link → `bounced_at`, `bounce_reason`, notification.
- `dl_expiry_tell()`: a link that ran out without an answer → one notification. `dl_fail()`: notification when a link is closed after wrong codes.
- `dl_problem($L)` → tone, label, detail, action; the card shows a banner with the number of links needing attention, the warning and text on the row, and "Send again" (posts `dl_send` with the same address).
- First message refused → nothing stored, the flash carries the reason.

**Tests (sandbox)**: card before the migration; refused first send; failing code mail → stored, notified, shown with "Send again"; `dl_bounce_match()` with three made-up inbox messages (a return naming the address → matched; an ordinary reply from the recipient → ignored; a return for another address → ignored); returned state shown with the text; expiry → notification and "No answer". **Not tested**: reading the real inbox through Microsoft 365.

**Security review**: the inbox is read with `$select` of subject, preview and sender only, nothing is stored except the matched preview (250 characters) on the link; only messages from mail systems are considered, so a customer's reply cannot mark a link as returned; reasons are shown escaped. Proposed: the same returned-message check for newsletters, to fill the unsubscribe list automatically.

Language keys 10 355 each.

## §346 v1.154.0 (R97-061) — Service Engineer app 0.7.0: customer acceptance

**Request**: "is this also integrated in the mobile versions?" → "I want it in the engineer app".

**How the app is delivered**: `customer/mobile/` is a web application; the store shells (Capacitor) load it from the portal (`server.url`) and the service worker replaces the shell when `VERSION` in `sw.js` changes. A portal release therefore updates the app — no store build. Version 0.6.21 → **0.7.0** (`sw.js`, `index.html`, `js/api.js`).

**API** (`api/v1`, engineer app; `sgn` and `dl` added to the module list in `bootstrap.php`):

- `GET /fsr/{id}/acceptance` → status, what is possible (`onsite`, `email`), the links with state and problem, the customer's contacts, the report in the app's shape.
- `POST /fsr/{id}/accept-link` {email, name} → `dl_create()`; `POST /fsr/{id}/accept-link/{link}/cancel` → `dl_cancel()`; `POST /fsr/{id}/sign-onsite` {decision, name, role, remark, signature, read} → `sgn_decide()`.
- `Portal::fsrMine()`: the report must be the engineer's own (author, submitter or on a time line), as in the report list; errors of the portal functions come back as 422 with their text.
- `shapeFsr()` adds `accepted_via`, `accepted_role`.
- Shared functions no longer redirect when there is no page session (`dl_staff()`, check in `sgn_decide()`); `dl_base()` gives the address of `customer/` also when called from `…/api/v1` (the link in the e-mail would otherwise have pointed into the API folder).

**App** (`mobile/js/reports.js`, `api.js`, `i18n.js` en / el / it, `css/app.css`): `acceptanceBox()` under a submitted report; `signScreen()` (`#/fsr/{id}?sign=1`, app chrome hidden, customer view = `view()` up to a cut mark, signature pad of the app); `previewScreen()` (`#/fsr/{id}?preview=1`) for a draft with on-site signing. Online only — these calls are never queued.

**Also**: a link is no longer shown as a problem when a newer link went to the same address; the warning banner appears only while the document still waits.

**Tests (sandbox)**: API — state, no token 401, another engineer 403, unknown report 404, bad address and missing mail 422, signing without signature / name 422, sign → accepted `on_site`, second signature 422, cancel. App on an emulated phone — sign-in, report list, acceptance box, send (refused here: no mail), signing screen with chrome hidden, three validation messages, drawing, "Signed — thank you", back to the report showing the signer; draft: preview and back to the form with "on site" still chosen; no script errors, no sideways scroll. **Not tested**: inside the installed iOS / Android shells, and with real outgoing mail.

**Security review**: same rules as the portal functions (they are the same functions); the app can act only on the engineer's own reports; API calls rate-limited (20 a minute for sending and signing); the token of a link never reaches the app; the customer screen hides the app's navigation, the session remains the engineer's. Proposed: require the phone's unlock (Face ID / fingerprint) again before the engineer's menu returns after the customer has held the phone.

**Numbering**: another session released GAS-CP-R97-060 / v1.153.0 (website inbox: Integrated Bridge questionnaire) while this work was being finished; this release was put on top of it and renumbered R97-061 / v1.154.0. That release has no § in this guide.

Language keys 10 358 each (portal; +3 from R97-060, none from this release); app keys +41 in three languages.

## §347 v1.155.0 (R97-063) — HR › Assessments

**Request**: "I need to create in the Hr a module to perform metrics through tests to measure different things such as reliability of an employee, consistency, willing to learn, willing to evolve, interest in new technologies and innovations … raised by HR Manager or CEO through the import of an excel … campaigns should run in the portal for specific periods and for specific target groups". Decisions: manager view added; results for the person (own), HR Manager and CEO; English, Greek, Italian, Bulgarian, Ukrainian; kept without expiry with statistics over the years per measurement; the rater is the department head; "the results will be automatically recorded to their annual evaluation as well".

**Template**: `GAS-2026-PORTAL-HRA-001_R0` (`customer/app/tpl/`, download for HR only: `?p=hr_tests&template=1`). Sheets Campaign (key / value), Dimensions, Scale (1–5), Questions (No, Form SELF / MANAGER, Dimension, Type LIKERT / SITUATION / CHOICE / CHECK, Reverse, Weight, Pair, Required, texts), Options, Bands. The header row of each sheet is found by its first cell; the table ends at the first empty row or at a row "Note".

**Code**: `customer/app/hrs.php` (prefix `hrs` — `hra` is the HR Assistant, `hrt` the training notifications). Tables (migration `r97x`): `hrs_campaigns` (the whole form as JSON in `form`, status draft / scheduled / running / closed), `hrs_targets` (one row per person: rater fixed at the start, answers, scores, flags), `hrs_scores` (the permanent record: campaign, person, family, year, dimension, self / manager / combined, department).

- Import: `hrs_parse()` → form or list of errors (nothing is stored on errors, the upload is never kept); `hrs_import_upload()`.
- Campaign: `hrs_targets_set()`, `hrs_rater_for()` (department head → own manager → nobody; never the person), `hrs_start()` / `hrs_begin()`, `hrs_remind()`, `hrs_close()`, `hrs_delete()` (draft only), `hrs_sweep()` (job `hr-assessments` in `nf.php`, hourly: start, reminders at half time and two days before the end, close the day after the last day).
- Answers: `hrs_order()` (statements mixed with a fixed order per person, then situations, then choices), `hrs_answer()` (save / submit, time per sitting), `hrs_clean_answers()`, `hrs_missing()`.
- Scoring `hrs_score()`: LIKERT (answer−1)/4, reversed (5−answer)/4; SITUATION points / highest points; CHOICE 1 to the dimension of the chosen statement, 0 to the other; CHECK not scored. Dimension = round(100·Σ(value·weight)/Σ(weight)); combined = (self·self_weight + manager·manager_weight)/(sum of weights), self alone without a manager rating. Low confidence: ≥ 2 contradictions, average time per question below `fast_seconds`, or the same answer on 90 % of the statements. The worked example of the sheet Methodology gives 83 / 63 / 73 / Solid in the portal as in the workbook.
- Evaluation: `hrs_eval_rows($pid, $year, $own)` (last closed campaign of each family in the year), `hrs_to5()`, `hrs_eval_scores()` stored as `as_<DIM>` in `hr_evaluations.scores` by `hr_evaluation_save_from_post()` after the overall mark is computed, `hrs_eval_record()` at closing for evaluations not yet acknowledged. Rows shown in `_hr_eval.php` to HR / CEO only and in `my_leave.php` to the person.
- Pages: `hr_tests`, `hr_test`, `hr_test_take`, `hr_test_result`, card `_hrs_cards.php` (My leave, HR requests).
